Let's formalize SOT iteration:
Given f (sum S). M = max f*f. Solve max 1^T g s.t. g>=0, K(g) <= M where K(g)_k = (f*g)_k. Since constraints linear. Extreme direction. Then g normalized sum=S. New f = (1-t)f + t g. This effectively moves mass from regions that increase peaks to those that are allowed. Could implement this LP direction exactly via efficient dual? The primal:
max c^T g (c=1)
s.t. T g <= M * 1, g>=0
where T is Toeplitz convolution with f: (T g)_k = sum_i f_i g_{k-i}. T nonnegative.
Dual:
min M * 1^T y
s.t. T^T y >= 1, y>=0
where T^T y at index i = sum_k f_{k-i} y_k, i.e. convolution y * f linear. This is same: find y>=0 minimizing M sum y s.t. f * y >= 1 pointwise. Then primal optimum = dual. So LP direction g corresponds to thresholding? If we can compute y, then KKT gives g support where (T^T y)_i > 1? Actually dual slack: g_i >0 => (T^T y)_i =1. Primal constraints tight where y_k>0. So active lag set Y support y. Need find y with min sum, constraints fuzzy convolution >=1. This is like covering by shifted copies of f. Could be solved by iterative water-filling? But maybe easier: Since f fixed, we seek y such that (f*y)_i ≥1. f is the current autocorrelation? Actually f sequence, not c. f values nonnegative. This is linear convolution inequality. Could solve via dual of previous. LP dimension similar.

But maybe the direction can be computed as greatest feasible g. This is equivalent to max total mass placed with cumulative constraint. Could use greedy because Toeplitz nonnegative but not trivial.

Could approximate direction by solving dual with projected gradient: minimize sum y s.t. f*y >=1, y>=0. This is convex. Then g from active set: set g_i positive where slack? KKT not direct. But maybe use y to define direction: reduce where f*y high? Hmm.

`gradient_lse_norm(a, beta)`:
```
n=len(a); S=a.sum(); C=math.sqrt(2*n)
q=a/S
cq=fast_conv(q) # length 2n-1
cs=C*C*cq
m=cq.max(); p=softmax(beta*(cs-m)? actually with cs max? Use cs for scale; p=softmax(beta*(cs - cs.max()))
# greedy conv: (rev_q * p)[n-1:2n-1]
M2=3*n-2; M2_fft=...; RQ=np.fft.rfft(q[::-1],M2_fft); P=np.fft.rfft(p,M2_fft); cr=np.fft.irfft(RQ*P,M2_fft)[n-1:2n-1]
# cr[j] = ∑_i q_i p_{i+j}
# g_q = 2*C^2 * cr? We can use cr - q*(q.dot(cr))
W = float((q*cr).sum())
g = (cr - q*W)  # maybe times constant
# true gradient has factor? We'll use 2*(cr - qW) maybe
grad = 2.0 * (cr - q*W)
return objvalue, grad
```

Let's implement `max_shave(a, max_iters, deadline)`:
```
a = clip, if sum<1e-12...
best=a.copy(); best_sc=exact_score(a); lower=1+1/(2n-1) maybe 1.0
alpha=None
for it:
  c=fast_conv(a); S=sum; M=max; k=argmax; score=2n*M/S^2
  if score<best...
  sg = zeros; v = derivative of c at k: for j in range(max(0,k-n+1), min(k,n-1)+1): sg[j]=2*a[k-j]
  S2=S*S; S3=S*S*S
  g=2*n*(sg/S2 - 2*M/S3)
  norm=np.linalg.norm(g)
  if norm<1e-15: break
  # initial step based on previous success or Polyak
  if alpha is None: alpha = min(1.0/(norm*S+eps), (score-1.0)/(norm*norm+eps), 1.0/S)
  for lin in 50:
    alpha_try=alpha
    a_trial=a - alpha_try*g
    a_trial=np.clip(a_trial,0,1000)
    S_new=a_trial.sum()
    if S_new<1e-12: alpha*=0.5; continue
    a_trial = a_trial * (S / S_new) # maintain sum? optional
    sc_trial=exact_score(a_trial)
    if sc_trial < score - 1e-18 * alpha_try * norm*norm: break # or just <score
    alpha*=0.5
    if alpha<1e-15: ...
  a = a_trial clip normalized
  alpha=max(alpha, 1e-8/(norm*S+eps))? 
```

`lse_norm_obj`:

def lse_norm_obj(a, beta):
    n=len(a); a=np.clip(a,0,None); S=a.sum()
    if S<=1e-15: return 1e100, zeros
    C2=2.0*n; C=math.sqrt(C2)
    q=a/S
    cq=fast_conv(q)
    cs=C2*cq  # sum C2
    m=cs.max()
    z=beta*(cs-m)
    z=np.clip(z,-700,700)
    ez=np.exp(z)
    ez_sum=ez.sum()
    p=ez/ez_sum
    L=m + np.log(ez_sum)/beta
    # cr
    pm=p.max()
    if pm > 1.0 - 1e-8:
       k=np.argmax(p)
       cr=np.zeros(n)
       js=max(0,k-n+1); je=min(k,n-1)
       for j in range(js,je+1): cr[j]=p[k]*q[k-j] # but p maybe not exactly 1; use top? 
       # if multiple near? Could use top mask p>1e-12 and if count small, loop; else FFT.
       # better: if np.sum(p>1e-10) < 100: loop over p nonneg indices:
       idx=np.nonzero(p>1e-10)[0]
       if len(idx)<256:
          cr=np.zeros(n)
          for k in idx:
             pw=p[k]
             if pw>0:
                js=...; cr[js:je+1] += pw * q[?][::-1]? Need q index k-j.
       else FFT.
    else FFT.
    W=float(np.dot(q,cr))
    grad = (2.0*C2/S)*(cr - q*W)
    return float(L), grad
```
But looping over each idx may be okay if few. For sparse p, can compute `cr[_] += pw * q[k - js:je+1]`? Need correct slice. For fixed k, cr[j]=∑_i q_i p_{i+j}; but if p one-hot at k, cr[j]=q_{k-j}. For j range [max(0,k-n+1), min(k,n-1)], q indices [k-je, k-js] reversed. So `cr[js:je+1] = q[k-je:k-js+1][::-1] * pw`. Good.
For multiple idx, do for each. If n 7k, idx<256, cost small.

But if p is not sparse, use FFT. For moderate beta, p maybe broad; FFT.

          a_t=np.clip(a_t,0,1000)

def interpolate_sequence(a, n_new):
    x_old=np.linspace(0,1,len(a)); y=a
    x_new=np.linspace(0,1,n_new)
    b=np.interp(x_new,x_old,y)
    b=np.clip(b,0,None)
    if sum=0: b=ones
    return b * (sqrt(2n_new)/sum)

def run_lbfgs_lse(a0, beta, deadline, maxfun=1000000, ftol=1e-18, gtol=1e-12, maxls=30):
   a0=np.clip(a0,0,1000)
   if a0.sum() <1e-12: ...
   MIN_STATE['deadline']=deadline
   bounds=[(0.0,1000.0)]*len(a0)
   try:
      res=minimize(lse_norm_obj, a0, args=(float(beta),), method='L-BFGS-B', jac=True, bounds=bounds,
                   options={'ftol': ftol, 'gtol': gtol, 'maxls': maxls, 'maxfun': maxfun, 'maxcor': 40},
                   callback=minimize_callback)
      out=np.clip(res.x,0,1000)
   except Exception:
      out=a0.copy()
   return normalize(out) # maybe maintain target
```

`max_subgrad` should be efficient. Let's design robust variant with soft weights and exact line search. We can use LSE p but objective exact line search. This is like subgradient of LSE (smooth) and should make progress.
Pseudo:
```
def max_subgrad_polish(a, beta, max_iters, deadline):
    a=normalize(a); n=len; best=a.copy(); bestsc=exact_score(a); alpha=None
    for it in range(max_iters):
       if time.monotonic()>=deadline: break
       c=fast_conv(a); S=a.sum(); if S<=... break
       Mg=c.max(); sc=2n*Mg/S^2
       if sc<bestsc: update
       q=a/S; C2=2n; cs=C2*c
       m_s=float(cs.max())
       z=beta*(cs-m_s); clip; ez; esum; p=ez/esum
       cr=conv_cr(q,p)
       W=float(np.dot(q,cr))
       grad=(2.0*C2/S)*(cr - q*W)
       gn=norm
       if gn<1e-14: break
       # If alpha None, use 1/(gn) relative? Since a scale C, step α.
       if alpha is None:
           # Try based on score gap to lower bound and grad norm
           alpha = min(1.0/(S*gn+1e-15), 1.0/(S+1e-15)*0.1, 0.5)
       # Line search
       success=False
       for _ in range(30):
          a_t=a - alpha*grad
          a_t=np.clip(a_t,0,1000)
          St=a_t.sum()
          if St>1e-12:
             a_t = a_t * (S / St)  # keep sum
          sc_t=exact_score(a_t)
          if sc_t < sc - 1e-12*max(1.0,sc):
             success=True; break
          alpha *= 0.5
          if alpha < 1e-16: break
       if not success:
          # Try exact small damped? break after too many fails
          if alpha < 1e-16 / S:
             break
          # don't move
          continue
       a=a_t
       # increase alpha adaptively but capped
       alpha = min(alpha*1.2, 0.5/(S+1e-15))
    return bestsc,best
```
Because grad is gradient of smooth H not exact score; line search on exact score may fail even if H descent. But H approx score at high β. For α small, exact score descent direction same if p active? For multipeak LSE, maybe. If failures, shrink. Could use H for line search to ensure descent; but we want exact. We can compute H_t? That needs another LSE; expensive. Maybe use `sc_t < sc` without margin and accept if no failure? To avoid cycles. We can do Armijo with previous value? Simpler: accept if sc_t < sc. If not, shrink. If no shrink enough, keep and maybe try reset alpha. This may make progress.

Could use step in log domain to guarantee? no.

Maybe implement `exact_peak_shave` single active with Polyak and Armijo. It may be faster because no p FFT? It still needs c FFT to find max and gradient for that k via slice (no second FFT). Very fast. We can run many iterations. Let's derive and implement.
```
def exact_peak_shave(a, max_iters, deadline, lower_bound=1.0):
   a=normalize(a); S=a.sum() target; n; best...
   alpha=None
   fail=0
   for it:
      c=fast_conv(a); S=a.sum(); M=c.max(); k=int(argmax(c)); sc=2n*M/S2
      if sc<best...
      if sc <= lower_bound*(1+1e-12): break
      # derivative sg for active k: d_j=2*a[k-j]
      sg=zeros; js=max(0,k-n+1); je=min(k,n-1); sg[js:je+1]=2*a[js:je+1][::-1]
      S2=S*S; S3=S2*S
      grad=2.0*n*(sg/S2 - 2.0*M/S3)
      gn=norm
      if gn<1e-15: break
      if alpha is None:
         # Polyak with floor
         alpha=(sc - lower_bound)/(gn*gn + 1e-30)
         alpha=min(alpha, 0.5/S)
         alpha=max(alpha, 1e-6/(gn*(S+1e-30)))
      # line search maybe reduce if exact score not decrease
      success=False
      for ls in range(20):
          at=a - alpha*grad
          at=clip; St=sum; if St>0: at*=S/St
          sc_t=exact_score(at)
          if sc_t < sc - 1e-15*sc: success=True; break
          alpha*=0.5
      if not success:
          # try only clip no normalize? or break after fail count
          fail+=1
          alpha*=0.5
          if fail>20 or alpha<1e-16/S: break
          continue
      a=at; fail=0; alpha=min(alpha*1.1, 1.0/S)
   return best
```
This uses only one FFT per iter (no p). Could run thousands. Exact subgradient non-smooth but good for peak shaving. We can also average top peaks to avoid single peak switching. But single max okay with line search; if multiple equal might oscillate. Soft version handles multiple with second FFT. We can implement `multi_peak_shave` using top-k uniform weights:

Gradient = 2n( (1/q) sg_sum/S^2 - 2(1/q)∑M_k/S^3)? Actually F=(C2/S^2)*(1/q)∑ c_k? Since score target max? If we minimize average active peaks, not max. But descent direction. More robust. Let's detail functions.

`normalize_target(a, target=None)`:
```
def normalize_target(a, target=None):
    a = np.asarray(a, dtype=np.float64).ravel()
    if a.size == 0:
        a = np.ones(64, dtype=np.float64)
    a = np.where(np.isfinite(a), a, 0.0)
    a = np.clip(a, 0.0, 1000.0)
    S = float(a.sum())
    n = a.size
    if target is None:
        target = math.sqrt(2.0 * n)
    if S <= 1e-14:
        a = np.ones(n, dtype=np.float64)
        S = n
    return a * (float(target) / S)
```
Clipping to 1000 before sum; if target bigger? target sqrt(2n) <= sqrt(40000)=200 for n=20000. fine.

`fast_conv(a)`:
```
def fast_conv(a):
    n=a.size
    if n==1: return a*a? Actually conv square [a0^2]
    M=2*n-1
    M_fft=1 << (M-1).bit_length()
    A=np.fft.rfft(a, M_fft)
    c=np.fft.irfft(A*A, M_fft, n=M_fft)
    c=c[:M]
    # remove tiny imaginary? irfft real
    if c.min() < 0.0:
       c = np.maximum(c, 0.0) # maybe only if max negative small
    return c
```
For n=1, bit_length? M=1, (M-1).bit_length()=0, 1<<0=1. ok. If a all zero, c zero.

`conv_cr(q,p)`:
```
def conv_cr(q,p):
    n=q.size
    if n==0: return np.zeros(0)
    # direct for small n
    if n <= 64:
       cr=np.zeros(n)
       for k,pw in enumerate(p):
         if pw>1e-14:
           js=max(0,k-n+1); je=min(k,n-1)
           if je>=js:
             cr[js:je+1] += pw * q[k-je:k-js+1][::-1]
       return cr
    nz_idx=np.flatnonzero(p > 1e-14)
    if nz_idx.size <= 128:
       cr=np.zeros(n)
       for k in nz_idx:
          pw=float(p[k])
          js=max(0,k-n+1); je=min(k,n-1)
          if je>=js:
             cr[js:je+1] += pw * q[k-je:k-js+1][::-1]
       return cr
    M2 = 3*n -2
    M2_fft = 1 << (M2-1).bit_length()
    RA=np.fft.rfft(q[::-1], M2_fft)
    P=np.fft.rfft(p, M2_fft)
    c=np.fft.irfft(RA*P, M2_fft)
    cr=c[n-1:2*n-1]
    # If roundoff, clip small? Don't clip all because can be negative? It should be nonnegative if p,q nonneg. But roundoff can negative tiny. We can max with 0? If p has negative? no. But if gradient, negative small okay. To preserve sum? Use cr = np.where(cr>0, cr, 0) maybe okay because true nonnegative; but if significant negative due roundoff? small. Do `cr = np.minimum(cr, None?)` No. `cr = np.where(cr < 0 & cr > -1e-12, 0, cr)` complex. Simpler `cr = np.maximum(cr,0.0)` because true nonnegative. This can slightly change gradient but stable.
    return np.maximum(cr, 0.0)
```
Threshold 1e-14 may include due p from exp normalized, sum1, rare many with >1e-14? length 14k, maybe not. If beta moderate, p broad; use FFT.

`lse_norm_obj(a,beta)`:
```
def lse_norm_obj(a, beta):
   n=int(len(a))
   a=np.asarray(a, dtype=np.float64).ravel()
   if a.size==0: return (1e100, np.zeros(0))
   a=np.clip(a,0,1000)
   S=float(a.sum())
   if S <= 1e-14:
      return (1e100, np.zeros(n))
   C2=2.0*n
   q=a/S
   c=fast_conv(q)
   if not np.any(c>0):
      return (C2*float(c.max())? maybe big, zeros) # if q zero impossible
   mc=float(c.max())
   betaq=beta*C2
   z=betaq*(c-mc)
   z=np.clip(z,-700,700)
   ez=np.exp(z)
   esum=float(ez.sum())
   if esum <= 0 or not finite: p zeros; p[argmax]=1; logp=0
   else: p=ez/esum; logp=math.log(esum) if esum>0 else 0
   L=C2*mc + logp/beta
   cr=conv_cr(q,p)
   W=float(np.dot(q,cr))
   grad=(2.0*C2/S)*(cr - q*W)
   # ensure no nan
   if not np.all(np.isfinite(grad)): grad=zeros; L=1e100
   return (float(L), grad)
```
Potential issue: If `beta` very large, betaq overflows? beta up to 1e6, C2 1e5 =>1e11, z multiplication float okay. c-mc ~1e-7, product ~1e4. Clip. Fine.
If `beta` is 0? We won't. If beta small, L nearly average, smooth. Good.
If c max and p uniform, L=C2*mc + log(2n-1)/β. Since C2*mc ~score. For β=5, maybe avg? L maybe >max? LSE≥max, okay.
For gradient, if p uniform over all, cr=average derivative? W=1/(2n) (average c_q). grad =2C2/S (averaged cr - q avg). This is gradient of squared? Fine.

`run_lbfgs_lse`:
Potential SciPy `minimize` with jac=True: It expects if jac is True, `fun` returns `(f, g)`. For args passed, `fun(x, *args)`. Our lse_norm_obj signature (a,beta). Good.
```
def run_lbfgs_lse(a,beta,deadline,maxfun=200000,ftol=1e-18,gtol=1e-12,maxls=20,maxcor=40):
   a=normalize_target(a)
   if time.monotonic() >= deadline: return a
   MIN_STATE['deadline']=deadline
   bounds=[(0.0,1000.0)]*len(a)
   try:
      res=minimize(lse_norm_obj, a, args=(float(beta),), method='L-BFGS-B', jac=True, bounds=bounds,
                   options={'ftol': ftol, 'gtol': gtol, 'maxls': maxls, 'maxfun': maxfun, 'maxcor': maxcor},
                   callback=minimize_callback)
      out=np.asarray(res.x, dtype=float)
      if out.size != len(a): out=a
   except Exception:
      out=a.copy()
   return normalize_target(out)
```
If callback stops early, maybe opt 'maxiter' not reached. fine.
Could set `gtol` adaptive: for high precision near 1.5, gtol 1e-14 may cause many iterations; use 1e-12. ftol 1e-16. L-BFGS may stop due ftol. Good.

`exact_score(a)` top-level:
```
def exact_score(a):
    a=np.asarray(a, dtype=float).ravel()
    n=a.size
    if n==0: return inf
    a=np.clip(a,0,1000)
    S=float(a.sum())
    if S<1e-12: return inf
    c=fast_conv(a)
    M=float(c.max())
    return float(2.0*n*M/(S*S))
```
If c all zeros, score 0? But S>0 and a nonnegative => M>0 unless n? If a one element, M=S^2, score=2*S^2/S^2=2.

`topm_sequence(a,m)`:
```
def topm_sequence(a,m):
   n=a.size; m=max(1,min(n,int(m)))
   if m==n: return normalize_target(a)
   order=np.argsort(a)[::-1][:m]
   order=np.sort(order)
   b=a[order]
   return normalize_target(b)
```
Could if m small and all equal? fine.

def block_sum(a, out_len):
   if out_len<16 or out_len>=a.size: return None
   # use cumulative sum at quantile edges to create out_len blocks

`interpolate_sequence(a,m)`:
```
def interpolate_sequence(a,m):
   n=a.size; m=max(16,min(20000, int(m)))
   if m==n: return normalize_target(a)
   x_old=np.linspace(0.0,1.0,n)
   x_new=np.linspace(0.0,1.0,m)
   b=np.interp(x_new, x_old, a)
   b=np.clip(b,0,None)
   if b.sum()<=1e-14: b=np.ones(m)
   return normalize_target(b)
```
For m larger, linearly smooths. For m smaller, averages.

`split_sequence(a)`:
```
def split_sequence(a):
   n=a.size
   b=np.empty(2*n)
   b[0::2]=a/2.0
   b[1::2]=a/2.0
   return normalize_target(b)
```
Could also alternate split with interpolation.

`exact_peak_shave` implementation details:
```
def exact_peak_shave(a, max_iters, deadline):
    a=normalize_target(a).astype(np.float64)
    n=a.size
    best=a.copy()
    best_sc=exact_score(a)
    lower=1.0 + 1.0/(2*n-1)  # theoretical avg lower bound (score)
    alpha=None
    fail=0
    C? no
    for it in range(max_iters):
        if time.monotonic() >= deadline: break
        S=float(a.sum())
        if S<=1e-14: break
        c=fast_conv(a)
        M=float(c.max()); k=int(np.argmax(c))
        sc=2.0*n*M/(S*S)
        if sc < best_sc:
            best_sc=sc; best=a.copy(); fail=0
        if sc <= lower*(1+1e-10): break
        js=max(0,k-n+1); je=min(k,n-1)
        sg=np.zeros(n)
        if je>=js:
            sg[js:je+1] = 2.0*a[js:je+1][::-1]
        S2=S*S; S3=S2*S
        grad=2.0*n*(sg/S2 - 2.0*M/S3)
        gn=float(np.linalg.norm(grad))
        if gn < 1e-16: break
        if alpha is None:
            # step based on lower bound and grad
            alpha = (sc - lower) / (gn*gn + 1e-300)
            alpha = min(alpha, 0.5 / S)
            alpha = max(alpha, 1e-7 / (gn * S + 1e-300))
        success=False
        for _ in range(30):
            at = a - alpha*grad
            at = np.clip(at, 0.0, 1000.0)
            St=float(at.sum())
            if St > 1e-14:
                at = at * (S / St)
            st = exact_score(at)
            if st < sc - 1e-14*max(1.0, sc):
                success=True; break
            alpha *= 0.5
            if alpha < 1e-18 / (S + 1e-300):
                break
        if not success:
            # try no normalization? If S drift maybe? no.
            # If α too small, break.
            if fail > 40 or alpha < 1e-16 / (S + 1e-300):
                break
            fail += 1
            # temporarily perturb? We can add tiny random? But deterministic. Maybe switch to smaller step.
            continue
        a = at
        fail=0
        # adapt alpha: if accepted, increase modestly but cap
        alpha = min(alpha * 1.2, 1.0 / (S + 1e-300))
    return best_sc,best
```
Line search uses exact_score which does another FFT (conv) each trial. Up to 30 trials -> 30k cost per accepted, expensive. Could reduce to 12 trials and use H? We can compute score cheaply? exact_score one FFT 16k. For 1000 iters with 5 trials =5k FFT, okay. 2400s maybe. Use 15 trials.
Could use `fast_conv` inside exact_score. We already computed c current; for trial can use exact FFT. Good.
Maybe line search can just take α and accept if sc_t < sc, without margin; halve. Fewer trials? 20.

`soft_factor_shave` similar but computes p and cr (two FFTs) each iter. Use fewer iterations (e.g., 300). It is smooth and can handle multipeaks. We can use for broader polish, exact for final.

Need if exact_peak_shave reduces score but best update only if improvement >. Good.

Potential alternative: Use `scipy.optimize.minimize` on exact maximum via `max()` and `scipy` subproblem? no.

Spectral perturb function:
```
def spectral_perturb(a, rng, amp=0.05):
   n=a.size; L=1<<ceil; A=np.fft.rfft(a,L); mag=np.abs(A); phase=np.angle(A); mag=mag*(1+amp*rng.standard_normal(L).clip(-2,2)); mag=np.maximum(mag,0); out=np.fft.irfft(mag*np.exp(1j*phase),L)[:n].real; out=np.maximum(out,0); return normalize(out)
```
No closures. Good.

```
def random_pattern(a_size, rng):
   kind=rng.integers(4)
   x=np.linspace(0,1,n)
   if kind==0: a=1+0.3*rng.normal(n)
   elif kind==1: xpow=rng.uniform(0.5,3); a=np.sin(np.pi*x)**xpow + 0.01
   elif kind==2: freq=rng.uniform(1,10); a=1+0.4*np.cos(2π freq x + phase)
   else: a=np.exp(-((x-loc)**2)/width) + 0.01 + noise
   clip normalize
```

Let's code `soft_factor_shave`:
```
def soft_factor_shave(a, beta, max_iters, deadline):
    a=normalize_target(a); n=a.size; best=a.copy(); best_sc=exact_score(a)
    alpha=None; fail=0
    for it in range(max_iters):
       if time>=deadline: break
       S=a.sum(); c=fast_conv(a); M; sc; update
       q=a/S; C2=2n; mc=c.max(); betaq=beta*C2; z=... p
       cr=conv_cr(q,p); W=dot; grad=(2C2/S)(cr-qW)
       gn=norm; if gn<1e-15 break
       if alpha is None: alpha=min(0.5/S, (sc-1)/(gn*gn+...), 0.2/S); alpha=max(alpha,1e-6/(gn*S+...))
       success=False
       for _ in range(25):
          at=a-alpha*grad; clip; St; normalize; st=exact_score(at)
          if st < sc - 1e-13*max(1,sc): success=True; break
          alpha*=0.5
       if not success: ...
       else: a=at; alpha=min(alpha*1.15,1/S)
    return best_sc,best
```
For initial alpha, since gradient components ~0.03, norm ~0.1? step 10? But cap 0.5/S ~0.004. Good.

def build_length_variants(base, max_n=20000):
    res=[]
    n=base.size
    # add if size within max and >=16 and not duplicate length maybe
    for frac in [...]:
       m=int(round(n*frac))
       if 16<=m<=max_n:
          try: res.append(topm_sequence(base,m))
          except: pass
    # merge pair
    if n//2>=16 and n//2<=max_n:
       try:
          if n%2==0: b=base.reshape(n//2,2).sum(1)
          else: b=base[:n-1].reshape((n-1)//2,2).sum(1)
          res.append(normalize_target(b))
          b2=base[1:].reshape((len(base[1:])//2),2).sum(1) if len(base[1:])%2==0 else ...
       res...
    # interpolate/split
    for m in [max(16,n//2), max(16, int(n*0.75)), max(16, int(n*1.25)), max(16, n*2)]:
       if m<=max_n: try res.append(interpolate_sequence(base,m)); except
    for m in [n*2]: if m<=max_n: try res.append(split_sequence(base)) except
    # deduplicate by length and sum? not necessary.
    return res
```

Let's code final scaling helper top-level `finalize_sequence(a)`:
```
def finalize_sequence(a):
    a=np.asarray(a,dtype=np.float64).ravel()
    a=np.where(np.isfinite(a), a, 0.0)
    a=np.clip(a,0,1000)
    S=float(a.sum()); maxv=float(np.max(a) if a.size else 0.0)
    if S <= 0.01:
        a=np.ones(64); S=64; maxv=1
    scale=10.0/S
    if maxv>0 and scale*maxv > 500.0:
        scale=500.0/maxv
    if scale*S < 1.0:
        scale=1.0/S
    out=a*scale
    if out.size==0 or out.sum()<=0.01:
        out=np.ones(64)*0.5
    if out.max()>1000:
        out=out*1000/out.max()
    if out.sum()<0.01:
        out=np.ones(64)*0.5
    return [float(x) for x in out]
```
This preserves score if scale uniform. If clipping? a already clipped. If scale due max 500, sum ~? If max dominates, sum ~500. Good. If max ratio makes sum <1, increase to sum1 may make max >500? We prioritize sum; max may become >500 but check final cap. If out.max()>1000 after sum scale, scale down; sum maybe still >0.01 because max huge implies sum huge. Good.

Now, we need ensure `topm_sequence` with m fraction after sorting values not too slow for many fractions (sort each time). We can sort once and prefix. To avoid nested helper? Top-level `topm_sequences(a, fractions)` could return list. More efficient.
```
def topm_sequences(a, fractions):
   n=a.size; idx=np.argsort(a)[::-1]
   res=[]
   seen=set()
   for frac in fractions:
      m=max(1,min(n,int(round(n*frac))))
      if m<16 or m in seen: continue
      seen.add(m)
      sub=np.sort(idx[:m])
      b=a[sub]
      res.append(normalize_target(b))
   return res
```
Top-level no closure. Good. Use in propose.

```
def merge_sequences(a):
   res=[]; n=a.size
   # half
   if n>=32:
       m=n//2
       if m>=16:
          b=a[:2*m].reshape(m,2).sum(axis=1)
          res.append(normalize_target(b))
          n2=(n-1)//2
          if n2>=16:
             b2=a[1:1+2*n2].reshape(n2,2).sum(axis=1)
             if n2!=m: res.append(normalize_target(b2))
   # quart
   m=n//4
   if m>=16:
       b=a[:4*m].reshape(m,4).sum(axis=1); res.append(normalize_target(b))
   return res
```
Good.

Now, `random_pattern` for restarts top-level:
```
def random_pattern(n, rng):
    x=np.linspace(0,1,n)
    kind=int(rng.integers(0,5))
    if kind==0:
       a=1.0 + 0.3*rng.standard_normal(n)
    elif kind==1:
       p=rng.uniform(0.5,3.0); a=np.sin(np.pi*x)**p + 0.01
    elif kind==2:
       f=rng.uniform(0.5,8.0); ph=rng.uniform(0,2*np.pi); a=1.0+0.6*np.cos(2*np.pi*f*x+ph)
    elif kind==3:
       loc=rng.uniform(0.1,0.9); w=rng.uniform(0.02,0.3); a=np.exp(-0.5*((x-loc)/w)**2)+0.01
    else:
       a=rng.random(n)**rng.uniform(1.0,5.0)+0.01
    a=np.clip(a,1e-6,None)
    return normalize_target(a)
```
Good.

`spectral_perturb` top-level:
```
def spectral_perturb(a, rng, amp=0.05):
    n=a.size
    L=1 << (2*n-2).bit_length()
    A=np.fft.fft(a, L)
    mag=np.abs(A)
    phase=np.angle(A)
    noise=np.clip(rng.standard_normal(L), -3,3)*amp
    mag=np.maximum(mag*(1.0+noise), 0.0)
    out=np.fft.ifft(mag*np.exp(1j*phase), L).real[:n]
    out=np.maximum(out,0.0)
    if out.sum()<=1e-12: out=np.ones(n)
    return normalize_target(out)
```
Good.

`log_perturb`:
```
def log_perturb(a, rng, sigma):
   a=normalize_target(a)
   pos=np.maximum(a, 1e-12)
   u=np.log(pos) + sigma*rng.standard_normal(len(a))
   u = u - u.mean()
   b=np.exp(u)
   return normalize_target(b)
```
If sigma large, exp overflow; u differences sigma*std normal ~0.1, safe. If sigma 0.3, differences ~2, exp diff 7 safe. Use clip.

def multiplicative_polish(a,beta,iters,deadline):
   a=normalize_target(a); best...

      q=a/S; c=fast_conv(q); p from LSE; cr=conv_cr; W; grad=(cr-qW) # direction to reduce L

To get z = beta*(c_s - max_s), we can compute `cs = c * (C2/(S*S))`. This may be O(n) but okay. Or compute q and cq with FFT since we need q for cr. We already have q. In our LSE gradient, cr=∑q_i p_{i+j}, W=q·cr. For single active, cr = q_{k-j} (valid) (not 2). Gradient formula =2C2/S(cr - q W), W=c_k(q). This is gradient wrt a. Let's adapt exact_peak_shave to q/LSE formula:
```
S=a.sum(); q=a/S; c=fast_conv(q); Mq=c.max(); k=argmax; sc=C2*Mq

W=float(np.dot(q,cr))  # Mq

```
This is exact gradient of score. Is it equivalent to previous? For S=C, factor 2C. Previous `grad=2n(sg_actual/S^2 -2M_actual/S^3)`. With sg_actual=2S*shift? Let's write soft_factor_shave in detail:
```
def soft_factor_shave(a, beta, max_iters, deadline):
    a = normalize_target(a).astype(np.float64); n=a.size; C2=2.0*n
    best=a.copy(); best_sc=score_sequence(a)
    alpha=None; fail=0
    lower = C2/(2*n-1)
    for _ in range(max_iters):
       if time.monotonic() >= deadline: break
       S=float(a.sum())
       if S <=1e-14: break
       q=a/S
       c=fast_conv(q)
       Mq=float(c.max()); sc=C2*Mq
       if sc < best_sc:
           best_sc=sc; best=a.copy(); fail=0
       if not np.isfinite(sc) or sc <= lower*(1+1e-12): break
       betaq=beta*C2
       z=betaq*(c-Mq)
       z=np.clip(z,-700,700); ez=np.exp(z); esum=float(ez.sum())
       if esum <=0 or not finite: break
       p=ez/esum
       cr=conv_cr(q,p)
       W=float(np.dot(q,cr))
       grad=(2.0*C2/S)*(cr - q*W)
       gn=float(np.linalg.norm(grad))
       if gn < 1e-15: break
       if alpha is None:
          alpha = min(0.2/S, (sc-lower)/(gn*gn+1e-30))
          alpha = max(alpha, 1e-7/(gn*S+1e-30))
       success=False
       for _ in range(25):
          at=a - alpha*grad
          if not np.all(np.isfinite(at)): alpha*=0.5; continue
          at=np.clip(at,0,1000)
          st=score_sequence(at)
          if np.isfinite(st) and st < sc - 1e-13*max(1.0, sc):
             success=True; break
          alpha *=0.5
          if alpha < 1e-18/(S+1e-30): break
       if not success:
          fail+=1
          if fail > 60: break
          continue
       a=at; fail=0
       alpha=min(alpha*1.15, 0.8/S)
    return best_sc,best
```
Note: Score sequence normalizes at, so no need normalize to S. It may use different scale but invariant. Good.

Implement `score_sequence` optimized:
```
def score_sequence(a):
    a = np.asarray(a, dtype=np.float64).ravel()
    n=a.size
    if n==0: return np.inf
    if not np.all(np.isfinite(a)):
       a=np.where(np.isfinite(a), a, 0.0)
    a=np.clip(a,0,1000)
    S=float(a.sum())
    if S <=1e-14: return np.inf
    c=fast_conv(a/S)
    return float(2.0*n*float(c.max()))
```

```python
"""
Search for non-negative step-function heights minimizing
    2*n*max(conv(a,a))/(sum(a))^2

The program combines:
  - length / support variants (top-m, merging, interpolation, splits),
  - scale-invariant log-sum-expectation (LSE) smoothing optimized by L-BFGS-B,
  - exact and soft peak-subgradient polishing with backtracking line searches,
  - basin-hopping restarts in log, spectral, and size-adapted spaces,
  - final verification against evaluate_sequence.
"""

import math
import time

import numpy as np
from scipy.optimize import minimize

MIN_STATE = {"deadline": 0.0}


def minimize_callback(x, k):
    if time.monotonic() >= MIN_STATE["deadline"]:
        return True
    return None


def normalize_target(a, target=None):
    a = np.asarray(a, dtype=np.float64).ravel()
    if a.size == 0:
        a = np.ones(64, dtype=np.float64)

    a = np.where(np.isfinite(a), a, 0.0)
    a = np.clip(a, 0.0, 1000.0)
    n = a.size
    S = float(a.sum())

    if target is None:
        target = math.sqrt(2.0 * n)
    target = float(target)

    if S <= 1e-14:
        a = np.ones(n, dtype=np.float64)
        S = float(n)

    return a * (target / S)


def fast_conv(a):
    a = np.asarray(a, dtype=np.float64).ravel()
    n = a.size
    if n == 0:
        return np.zeros(0, dtype=np.float64)
    M = 2 * n - 1
    M_fft = 1 << (M - 1).bit_length()
    A = np.fft.rfft(a, M_fft)
    c = np.fft.irfft(A * A, M_fft)
    c = c[:M]
    if c.size and float(c.min()) < 0.0:
        c = np.maximum(c, 0.0)
    return c


def conv_cr(q, p):
    """
    For q length n and p length 2n-1, return cr[j] = sum_i q[i] p[i+j]
    where valid indices are used. This is the convolution part of the
    normalized LSE gradient.
    """
    q = np.asarray(q, dtype=np.float64).ravel()
    p = np.asarray(p, dtype=np.float64).ravel()
    n = q.size
    if n == 0 or p.size != 2 * n - 1:
        return np.zeros(n, dtype=np.float64)

    if n <= 64:
        cr = np.zeros(n, dtype=np.float64)
        for k in range(p.size):
            pw = float(p[k])
            if pw <= 1e-15:
                continue
            js = max(0, k - n + 1)
            je = min(k, n - 1)
            if je >= js:
                cr[js:je + 1] += pw * q[k - je:k - js + 1][::-1]
        return np.maximum(cr, 0.0)

    nz = np.flatnonzero(p > 1e-14)
    if nz.size <= 128:
        cr = np.zeros(n, dtype=np.float64)
        for k in nz:
            pw = float(p[k])
            if pw <= 0.0:
                continue
            js = max(0, k - n + 1)
            je = min(k, n - 1)
            if je >= js:
                cr[js:je + 1] += pw * q[k - je:k - js + 1][::-1]
        return np.maximum(cr, 0.0)

    M2 = 3 * n - 2
    M2_fft = 1 << (M2 - 1).bit_length()
    RA = np.fft.rfft(q[::-1], M2_fft)
    P = np.fft.rfft(p, M2_fft)
    c = np.fft.irfft(RA * P, M2_fft)
    cr = c[n - 1:2 * n - 1]
    return np.maximum(cr, 0.0)


def score_sequence(a):
    """Exact internal equivalent of evaluate_sequence (scale-invariant form)."""
    a = np.asarray(a, dtype=np.float64).ravel()
    n = a.size
    if n == 0:
        return np.inf
    a = np.where(np.isfinite(a), a, 0.0)
    a = np.clip(a, 0.0, 1000.0)
    S = float(a.sum())
    if S <= 1e-14:
        return np.inf
    c = fast_conv(a / S)
    if c.size == 0:
        return np.inf
    return float(2.0 * n * float(c.max()))


def lse_norm_obj(a, beta):
    """
    Scale-invariant LSE objective:
        H(a) = LSE_beta( (2n) * conv(a/sum(a), a/sum(a)) )
    and its gradient with respect to a.
    """
    a = np.asarray(a, dtype=np.float64).ravel()
    n = a.size
    if n == 0:
        return 1e100, np.zeros(0, dtype=np.float64)

    a = np.where(np.isfinite(a), a, 0.0)
    a = np.clip(a, 0.0, 1000.0)
    S = float(a.sum())
    if S <= 1e-14:
        return 1e100, np.zeros(n, dtype=np.float64)

    C2 = 2.0 * n
    q = a / S
    c = fast_conv(q)
    if c.size == 0:
        return 1e100, np.zeros(n, dtype=np.float64)

    mc = float(c.max())
    betaq = float(beta) * C2

    z = betaq * (c - mc)
    z = np.clip(z, -700.0, 700.0)
    ez = np.exp(z)
    esum = float(ez.sum())

    if esum <= 1e-300 or not np.isfinite(esum):
        p = np.zeros(c.size, dtype=np.float64)
        p[int(np.argmax(c))] = 1.0
        logp = 0.0
    else:
        p = ez / esum
        logp = math.log(esum)

    val = C2 * mc + logp / float(beta)

    cr = conv_cr(q, p)
    W = float(np.dot(q, cr))
    grad = (2.0 * C2 / S) * (cr - q * W)

    if not np.all(np.isfinite(grad)):
        grad = np.zeros(n, dtype=np.float64)
        val = 1e100

    return float(val), grad


def run_lbfgs_lse(a, beta, deadline, maxfun=200000, ftol=1e-16, gtol=1e-12, maxls=30):
    a = normalize_target(a)
    if time.monotonic() >= deadline:
        return a

    MIN_STATE["deadline"] = float(deadline)
    bounds = [(0.0, 1000.0)] * len(a)

    try:
        res = minimize(
            lse_norm_obj,
            a,
            args=(float(beta),),
            method="L-BFGS-B",
            jac=True,
            bounds=bounds,
            options={
                "ftol": ftol,
                "gtol": gtol,
                "maxls": maxls,
                "maxfun": maxfun,
                "maxiter": 500000,
                "maxcor": 50,
            },
            callback=minimize_callback,
        )
        out = np.asarray(res.x, dtype=np.float64)
        if out.size != len(a):
            out = a.copy()
    except Exception:
        out = a.copy()

    out = np.where(np.isfinite(out), out, 0.0)
    out = np.clip(out, 0.0, 1000.0)
    return normalize_target(out)


def topm_sequences(a, fractions):
    res = []
    n = a.size
    if n <= 0:
        return res
    order = np.argsort(a)[::-1]
    seen = set()
    base = np.asarray(a, dtype=np.float64).ravel()
    for frac in fractions:
        m = int(round(n * float(frac)))
        m = max(1, min(n, m))
        if m < 16 or m in seen:
            continue
        seen.add(m)
        idx = np.sort(order[:m])
        res.append(normalize_target(base[idx]))
    return res


def merge_sequences(a):
    res = []
    a = np.asarray(a, dtype=np.float64).ravel()
    n = a.size
    if n < 64:
        return res

    m2 = n // 2
    if m2 >= 16:
        b = a[:2 * m2].reshape(m2, 2).sum(axis=1)
        res.append(normalize_target(b))

        m2b = (n - 1) // 2
        if m2b >= 16 and 1 + 2 * m2b <= n:
            b2 = a[1:1 + 2 * m2b].reshape(m2b, 2).sum(axis=1)
            if m2b != m2:
                res.append(normalize_target(b2))

    m4 = n // 4
    if m4 >= 16:
        b4 = a[:4 * m4].reshape(m4, 4).sum(axis=1)
        res.append(normalize_target(b4))

    return res


def interpolate_sequence(a, m):
    a = np.asarray(a, dtype=np.float64).ravel()
    n = a.size
    if n <= 0:
        return np.ones(64, dtype=np.float64)
    m = max(16, min(20000, int(m)))
    if m == n:
        return normalize_target(a)
    x_old = np.linspace(0.0, 1.0, n, dtype=np.float64)
    x_new = np.linspace(0.0, 1.0, m, dtype=np.float64)
    b = np.interp(x_new, x_old, a.astype(np.float64))
    b = np.where(np.isfinite(b), b, 0.0)
    b = np.maximum(b, 0.0)
    if float(b.sum()) <= 1e-14:
        b = np.ones(m, dtype=np.float64)
    return normalize_target(b)


def split_sequence(a):
    a = np.asarray(a, dtype=np.float64).ravel()
    n = a.size
    b = np.empty(2 * n, dtype=np.float64)
    b[0::2] = 0.5 * a
    b[1::2] = 0.5 * a
    return normalize_target(b)


def random_permutation(base, rng):
    base = np.asarray(base, dtype=np.float64).ravel()
    if rng is None:
        return normalize_target(base)
    perm = rng.permutation(base.size)
    return normalize_target(base[perm])


def build_length_variants(base, max_n=20000, rng=None):
    base = np.asarray(base, dtype=np.float64).ravel()
    n = base.size
    res = []

    fracs = (
        0.995, 0.99, 0.985, 0.98, 0.97, 0.96, 0.95, 0.94, 0.93,
        0.92, 0.91, 0.90, 0.88, 0.85, 0.80, 0.75, 0.70,
        0.60, 0.50, 0.40, 0.30, 0.25, 0.20
    )
    useful_fracs = []
    for f in fracs:
        m = int(round(n * f))
        if 16 <= m <= max_n:
            useful_fracs.append(f)
    if useful_fracs:
        res.extend(topm_sequences(base, useful_fracs))

    res.extend(merge_sequences(base))

    interp_lengths = set()
    for m in (
        n // 2,
        int(0.75 * n),
        int(1.10 * n),
        int(1.25 * n),
        int(1.50 * n),
        2 * n,
    ):
        m = int(m)
        if 16 <= m <= max_n and m not in interp_lengths:
            interp_lengths.add(m)
            res.append(interpolate_sequence(base, m))

    if 2 * n <= max_n and 2 * n >= 32:
        res.append(split_sequence(base))

    if rng is not None and n <= max_n:
        for _ in range(2):
            res.append(random_permutation(base, rng))

    # Cap total number of cheap variants.
    return res[:36]


def random_pattern(n, rng):
    n = max(32, int(n))
    x = np.linspace(0.0, 1.0, n, dtype=np.float64)
    kind = int(rng.integers(0, 5))
    if kind == 0:
        a = 1.0 + 0.35 * rng.standard_normal(n)
    elif kind == 1:
        p = float(rng.uniform(0.5, 3.0))
        a = np.sin(np.pi * x) ** p + 0.02
    elif kind == 2:
        f = float(rng.uniform(0.5, 8.0))
        ph = float(rng.uniform(0.0, 2.0 * np.pi))
        a = 1.0 + 0.6 * np.cos(2.0 * np.pi * f * x + ph)
    elif kind == 3:
        loc = float(rng.uniform(0.1, 0.9))
        w = float(rng.uniform(0.02, 0.30))
        a = np.exp(-0.5 * ((x - loc) / w) ** 2) + 0.02
    else:
        gamma = float(rng.uniform(1.0, 5.0))
        a = rng.random(n) ** gamma + 0.02

    a = np.where(np.isfinite(a), a, 0.0)
    a = np.maximum(a, 1e-9)
    return normalize_target(a)


def spectral_perturb(a, rng, amp=0.05):
    a = np.asarray(a, dtype=np.float64).ravel()
    n = a.size
    if n < 2:
        return normalize_target(a)
    N = max(64, 2 * n - 1)
    L = 1 << (N - 1).bit_length()
    A = np.fft.fft(a.astype(np.float64), L)
    mag = np.abs(A)
    phase = np.angle(A)
    noise = np.clip(rng.standard_normal(L), -3.0, 3.0) * float(amp)
    mag = np.maximum(mag * (1.0 + noise), 0.0)
    out = np.fft.ifft(mag * np.exp(1j * phase), L).real[:n]
    out = np.where(np.isfinite(out), out, 0.0)
    out = np.maximum(out, 0.0)
    if float(out.sum()) <= 1e-14:
        out = np.ones(n, dtype=np.float64)
    return normalize_target(out)


def log_perturb(a, rng, sigma):
    a = normalize_target(a)
    n = a.size
    pos = np.maximum(a, 1e-12)
    u = np.log(pos) + float(sigma) * rng.standard_normal(n)
    u = u - float(u.mean())
    u = np.clip(u, -50.0, 50.0)
    b = np.exp(u)
    b = np.where(np.isfinite(b), b, 0.0)
    b = np.maximum(b, 0.0)
    if float(b.sum()) <= 1e-14:
        b = np.ones(n, dtype=np.float64)
    return normalize_target(b)


def exact_peak_shave(a, max_iters, deadline):
    a = normalize_target(a).astype(np.float64)
    n = a.size
    C2 = 2.0 * n
    lower = C2 / (2.0 * n - 1.0)

    best = a.copy()
    best_sc = score_sequence(a)

    alpha = None
    fail = 0

    for _ in range(int(max_iters)):
        if time.monotonic() >= deadline:
            break

        S = float(a.sum())
        if S <= 1e-14:
            break
        q = a / S
        c = fast_conv(q)
        Mq = float(c.max())
        sc = float(C2 * Mq)

        if not np.isfinite(sc):
            break
        if sc < best_sc:
            best_sc = sc
            best = a.copy()
            fail = 0

        if sc <= lower * (1.0 + 1e-12):
            break

        k = int(np.argmax(c))
        js = max(0, k - n + 1)
        je = min(k, n - 1)

        cr = np.zeros(n, dtype=np.float64)
        if je >= js:
            cr[js:je + 1] = q[js:je + 1][::-1]

        W = Mq
        grad = (2.0 * C2 / S) * (cr - q * W)
        gn = float(np.linalg.norm(grad))

        if not np.isfinite(gn) or gn < 1e-16:
            break

        if alpha is None:
            alpha = (sc - lower) / (gn * gn + 1e-300)
            alpha = min(alpha, 0.3 / S)
            alpha = max(alpha, 1e-6 / (gn * S + 1e-300))

        success = False
        for _ in range(15):
            at = a - alpha * grad
            if not np.all(np.isfinite(at)):
                alpha *= 0.5
                continue
            at = np.clip(at, 0.0, 1000.0)
            st = score_sequence(at)
            if np.isfinite(st) and st < sc - 1e-13 * max(1.0, sc):
                success = True
                break
            alpha *= 0.5
            if alpha < 1e-18 / (S + 1e-300):
                break

        if not success:
            fail += 1
            if fail > 60 or alpha < 1e-16 / (S + 1e-300):
                break
            continue

        a = at
        fail = 0
        alpha = min(alpha * 1.18, 0.9 / S)

    return float(best_sc), best


def soft_factor_shave(a, beta, max_iters, deadline):
    a = normalize_target(a).astype(np.float64)
    n = a.size
    C2 = 2.0 * n
    lower = C2 / (2.0 * n - 1.0)

    best = a.copy()
    best_sc = score_sequence(a)

    alpha = None
    fail = 0

    for _ in range(int(max_iters)):
        if time.monotonic() >= deadline:
            break

        S = float(a.sum())
        if S <= 1e-14:
            break

        q = a / S
        c = fast_conv(q)
        Mq = float(c.max())
        sc = float(C2 * Mq)

        if not np.isfinite(sc):
            break
        if sc < best_sc:
            best_sc = sc
            best = a.copy()
            fail = 0

        if sc <= lower * (1.0 + 1e-12):
            break

        betaq = float(beta) * C2
        z = betaq * (c - Mq)
        z = np.clip(z, -700.0, 700.0)
        ez = np.exp(z)
        esum = float(ez.sum())

        if esum <= 1e-300 or not np.isfinite(esum):
            break

        p = ez / esum
        cr = conv_cr(q, p)
        W = float(np.dot(q, cr))
        grad = (2.0 * C2 / S) * (cr - q * W)
        gn = float(np.linalg.norm(grad))

        if not np.isfinite(gn) or gn < 1e-16:
            break

        if alpha is None:
            alpha = (sc - lower) / (gn * gn + 1e-300)
            alpha = min(alpha, 0.25 / S)
            alpha = max(alpha, 1e-7 / (gn * S + 1e-300))

        success = False
        for _ in range(15):
            at = a - alpha * grad
            if not np.all(np.isfinite(at)):
                alpha *= 0.5
                continue
            at = np.clip(at, 0.0, 1000.0)
            st = score_sequence(at)
            if np.isfinite(st) and st < sc - 1e-13 * max(1.0, sc):
                success = True
                break
            alpha *= 0.5
            if alpha < 1e-18 / (S + 1e-300):
                break

        if not success:
            fail += 1
            if fail > 80:
                break
            continue

        a = at
        fail = 0
        alpha = min(alpha * 1.15, 0.8 / S)

    return float(best_sc), best


def finalize_sequence(a):
    a = np.asarray(a, dtype=np.float64).ravel()
    a = np.where(np.isfinite(a), a, 0.0)
    a = np.clip(a, 0.0, 1000.0)

    S = float(a.sum())
    mx = float(a.max()) if a.size else 0.0

    if a.size == 0 or S <= 0.01:
        return [0.5] * 100

    scale = 10.0 / S
    if mx > 0.0 and scale * mx > 500.0:
        scale = 500.0 / mx
    if scale * S < 1.0:
        scale = 1.0 / S

    out = a * scale
    out = np.where(np.isfinite(out), out, 0.0)
    out = np.clip(out, 0.0, 1000.0)

    if out.sum() <= 0.01:
        out = np.ones(100, dtype=np.float64) * 0.5

    if out.size and out.max() > 1000.0:
        out = out * (1000.0 / out.max())

    return [float(x) for x in out]


def propose_candidate(seed=20260602):
    t_start = time.monotonic()
    total_s = 2350.0
    finish_limit = t_start + 2380.0
    rng = np.random.default_rng(seed)

    best_a = None
    parent = globals().get("height_sequence_1")
    if parent is not None:
        try:
            p = np.asarray(parent, dtype=np.float64).ravel()
            if p.size >= 16:
                best_a = normalize_target(p)
        except Exception:
            best_a = None

    if best_a is None:
        best_a = random_pattern(4096, rng)

    best_sc = score_sequence(best_a)
    if not np.isfinite(best_sc):
        best_a = random_pattern(4096, rng)
        best_sc = score_sequence(best_a)

    print(f"Initial: score={best_sc:.10f}, length={len(best_a)}, time={time.monotonic()-t_start:.1f}s")

    # Phase 0: cheap length / support variants.
    variant_deadline = min(t_start + min(70.0, 0.03 * total_s), finish_limit - 600.0)
    base_for_variants = best_a.copy()
    try:
        variants = build_length_variants(base_for_variants, max_n=20000, rng=rng)
    except Exception:
        variants = []

    for v in variants:
        if time.monotonic() >= variant_deadline:
            break
        try:
            scv = score_sequence(v)
        except Exception:
            continue
        if np.isfinite(scv) and scv < best_sc:
            best_sc = scv
            best_a = np.asarray(v, dtype=np.float64).copy()
            print(f"  Variant improved: {best_sc:.10f}, length={len(best_a)}")

    # Phase 1: LSE continuation via L-BFGS-B.
    main_start = time.monotonic()
    main_end = min(t_start + 0.62 * total_s, finish_limit - 320.0)

    a_cur = best_a.copy()
    cur_sc = best_sc

    schedule = [
        10.0, 20.0, 50.0, 100.0, 300.0, 1000.0, 3000.0, 10000.0, 30000.0, 100000.0,
    ]
    weights = [1.0, 1.0, 2.0, 3.0, 5.0, 8.0, 10.0, 12.0, 15.0, 8.0]
    total_weight = float(sum(weights))
    main_budget = max(0.0, main_end - main_start)
    t_alloc = main_start

    print(f"Main LSE phases allocated until t={main_end - t_start:.1f}s")
    for beta, w in zip(schedule, weights):
        if t_alloc >= main_end - 1.0:
            break
        delta = main_budget * (float(w) / total_weight)
        dl = min(t_alloc + delta, main_end)

        if dl - time.monotonic() < 2.0:
            t_alloc = max(t_alloc, time.monotonic())
            continue

        try:
            a_opt = run_lbfgs_lse(a_cur, beta, dl)
        except Exception:
            t_alloc = max(t_alloc, time.monotonic())
            continue

        if a_opt.size != a_cur.size:
            a_opt = a_cur.copy()

        try:
            sc = score_sequence(a_opt)
        except Exception:
            sc = np.inf

        t_alloc = max(t_alloc, time.monotonic())

        if np.isfinite(sc) and sc < best_sc:
            best_sc = sc
            best_a = np.asarray(a_opt, dtype=np.float64).copy()
            print(f"  LSE beta={beta:g}: NEW BEST {best_sc:.10f}, length={len(best_a)}")

        keep_tol = 5e-4 if beta < 50.0 else 1e-7
        if np.isfinite(sc) and sc < cur_sc + keep_tol:
            a_cur = a_opt
            cur_sc = sc
        else:
            a_cur = best_a.copy()
            cur_sc = best_sc

    # Phase 2: soft multi-peak polishing.
    soft_end = min(t_start + 0.78 * total_s, finish_limit - 210.0)
    if time.monotonic() < soft_end:
        a_pol, sc_pol = soft_factor_shave(best_a, beta=2500.0, max_iters=1000000, deadline=soft_end)
        if np.isfinite(sc_pol) and sc_pol < best_sc:
            best_sc = sc_pol
            best_a = np.asarray(a_pol, dtype=np.float64).copy()
            print(f"  Soft polish improved: {best_sc:.10f}")

    # Phase 3: exact peak shaving.
    exact_end = min(t_start + 0.84 * total_s, finish_limit - 160.0)
    if time.monotonic() < exact_end:
        a_pol, sc_pol = exact_peak_shave(best_a, max_iters=1000000, deadline=exact_end)
        if np.isfinite(sc_pol) and sc_pol < best_sc:
            best_sc = sc_pol
            best_a = np.asarray(a_pol, dtype=np.float64).copy()
            print(f"  Exact polish improved: {best_sc:.10f}")

    # Another soft pass if time remains.
    soft2_end = min(t_start + 0.88 * total_s, finish_limit - 120.0)
    if time.monotonic() < soft2_end:
        a_pol, sc_pol = soft_factor_shave(best_a, beta=1000.0, max_iters=1000000, deadline=soft2_end)
        if np.isfinite(sc_pol) and sc_pol < best_sc:
            best_sc = sc_pol
            best_a = np.asarray(a_pol, dtype=np.float64).copy()
            print(f"  Second soft polish improved: {best_sc:.10f}")

    # Phase 4: restarts / basin hopping.
    restart_end = min(t_start + 0.95 * total_s, finish_limit - 70.0)
    restart_count = 0
    max_restarts = 32

    while time.monotonic() < restart_end and restart_count < max_restarts:
        now = time.monotonic()
        remaining = restart_end - now
        if remaining < 25.0:
            break

        strategy = restart_count % 6
        n_best = len(best_a)

        if strategy == 0:
            a_p = log_perturb(best_a, rng, 0.015 + 0.035 * float(rng.random()))
            lse_beta = 5000.0
            soft_beta = 2000.0
        elif strategy == 1:
            a_p = spectral_perturb(best_a, rng, 0.045)
            lse_beta = 5000.0
            soft_beta = 2000.0
        elif strategy == 2:
            n_fresh = max(512, min(8192, n_best // 2))
            a_p = random_pattern(n_fresh, rng)
            lse_beta = 150.0
            soft_beta = 500.0
        elif strategy == 3:
            m = max(32, int(0.90 * n_best))
            try:
                a基板 = topm_sequences(best_a, [float(m) / float(n_best)])
                a_p = a基板[0] if len(a基板) else best_a.copy()
            except Exception:
                a_p = best_a.copy()
            a_p = log_perturb(a_p, rng, 0.02 + 0.03 * float(rng.random()))
            lse_beta = 3000.0
            soft_beta = 1500.0
        elif strategy == 4:
            a_p = random_permutation(best_a, rng)
            lse_beta = 2000.0
            soft_beta = 1000.0
        else:
            longer = min(20000, 2 * n_best)
            if longer <= n_best:
                longer = min(20000, n_best + 1024)
            a_p = interpolate_sequence(best_a, longer)
            lse_beta = 150.0
            soft_beta = 500.0

        try:
            sc_p = score_sequence(a_p)
        except Exception:
            sc_p = np.inf
        if np.isfinite(sc_p) and sc_p < best_sc:
            best_sc = sc_p
            best_a = np.asarray(a_p, dtype=np.float64).copy()
            print(f"  Restart raw improved: {best_sc:.10f}")

        a_pol = None
        now2 = time.monotonic()
        if a_p.size and time.monotonic() < restart_end - 12.0:
            chunk_left = restart_end - now2
            if a_p.size <= 9000 and chunk_left > 45.0:
                dl = min(restart_end, now2 + max(15.0, min(35.0, 0.35 * chunk_left)))
                try:
                    a_pol = run_lbfgs_lse(a_p, lse_beta, dl)
                    sc = score_sequence(a_pol)
                    if np.isfinite(sc) and sc < best_sc:
                        best_sc = sc
                        best_a = np.asarray(a_pol, dtype=np.float64).copy()
                        print(f"  Restart L-BFGS improved: {best_sc:.10f}")
                except Exception:
                    a_pol = a_p.copy()
            else:
                dl = min(restart_end, now2 + max(10.0, min(20.0, 0.35 * chunk_left)))
                try:
                    a_pol, sc = soft_factor_shave(a_p, soft_beta, 5000, dl)
                    if np.isfinite(sc) and sc < best_sc:
                        best_sc = sc
                        best_a = np.asarray(a_pol, dtype=np.float64).copy()
                        print(f"  Restart soft improved: {best_sc:.10f}")
                except Exception:
                    a_pol = a_p.copy()

        if a_pol is None:
            a_pol = a_p.copy()

        if time.monotonic() < restart_end - 8.0:
            dl_ex = min(restart_end, time.monotonic() + max(4.0, min(12.0, 0.15 * (restart_end - time.monotonic()))))
            try:
                _, sc_ex = exact_peak_shave(a_pol, 1000000, dl_ex)
                if np.isfinite(sc_ex) and sc_ex < best_sc:
                    best_sc = sc_ex
                    best_a = np.asarray(a_pol, dtype=np.float64).copy()
                    print(f"  Restart exact improved: {best_sc:.10f}")
            except Exception:
                pass

        restart_count += 1

    # Phase 5: final high-beta LSE and exact shave.
    final_end = min(t_start + 2378.0, finish_limit - 12.0)
    if time.monotonic() < final_end - 35.0:
        try:
            a_final = run_lbfgs_lse(best_a, 50000.0, final_end - 25.0)
            sc = score_sequence(a_final)
            if np.isfinite(sc) and sc < best_sc:
                best_sc = sc
                best_a = np.asarray(a_final, dtype=np.float64).copy()
                print(f"  Final LSE improved: {best_sc:.10f}")
        except Exception:
            pass

    if time.monotonic() < final_end - 4.0:
        try:
            a_final, sc_final = exact_peak_shave(best_a, 1000000, final_end)
            if np.isfinite(sc_final) and sc_final < best_sc:
                best_sc = sc_final
                best_a = np.asarray(a_final, dtype=np.float64).copy()
                print(f"  Final exact polish improved: {best_sc:.10f}")
        except Exception:
            pass

    out = finalize_sequence(best_a)

    verified = None
    try:
        verified = float(evaluate_sequence(out))
    except Exception:
        verified = None

    elapsed = time.monotonic() - t_start
    print(f"\nFINAL: internal={best_sc:.10f}, verified={verified}, length={len(out)}, elapsed={elapsed:.1f}s")

    if verified is None or not np.isfinite(verified) or verified > 1e9:
        # Extremely defensive fallback; should almost never be used.
        return [0.5] * 100

    return out
```
